===== apriori/specs/config/spec.md =====
### Requirement: process-config parses as structure, never as full-text regex
A shared reader (`lib/config.js`) SHALL be the single entry for every process-config consumer: it scans line-by-line, treating fenced code blocks (``` open/close; an unterminated fence makes the rest of the file inert) and HTML comments (`<!-- … -->`, multi-line; unterminated likewise inert; first-open wins, no nesting) as non-content; a config row is any `|`-leading table row — first cell key, second cell value, extra columns ignored, header and separator rows skipped; duplicate rows with the same key and value are tolerated silently, while different values for one key are a CONFLICT problem. Config problems surface ONLY when the key is actually consumed. The `--no-cas` flag keeps explicit supremacy over any config state.

#### Scenario: CF-01 fenced and commented rows never take effect
- WHEN process-config carries `| cas | optional |` inside a fenced block (or an HTML comment) and `| cas | required |` as a live row, with an unstamped MODIFIED delta being archived
- THEN the archive is DENIED (the live `required` wins; the fenced example grants nothing) — and the reversed layout (fenced `required`, live `optional`) waives with the waiver named

#### Scenario: CF-02 multi-column rows parse by their first two cells
- WHEN a live row reads `| cas | optional | 注释说明 |` (template-style extra column)
- THEN the key/value parse as cas/optional and the extra cell is ignored

#### Scenario: CF-03 duplicates tolerate sameness and refuse conflict
- WHEN one config carries `| cas | optional |` twice, and another carries `| cas | optional |` plus `| cas | required |`
- THEN the first reads cleanly as optional; the second is a CONFLICT — at consumption the archive errors (exit 1, nothing written) and gate C7 blocks naming the config conflict, never treating it as a waiver

#### Scenario: CF-04 unterminated blocks are inert, not effective
- WHEN a fence opens and never closes before rows that would otherwise waive CAS (same for an unterminated HTML comment)
- THEN those rows grant nothing — the file's tail is inert and an unstamped mutation archive is denied

#### Scenario: CF-05 config errors surface only at consumption
- WHEN the config carries a cas CONFLICT but the archived change is fully stamped (or ADDED-only)
- THEN the archive proceeds normally — the cas key was never consulted, so the bad row is invisible to this run

#### Scenario: CF-06 the waiver is discoverable
- WHEN `apriori archive` and `apriori gate` print their usage
- THEN both list `--no-cas`

#### Scenario: CF-07 the template names the cas row
- WHEN `templates/process-config.md` is read
- THEN it carries a `cas` row example documenting required-vs-optional semantics

===== apriori/specs/gate/spec.md =====
### Requirement: gate aggregates the mechanical exit conditions for one change
`apriori gate --change <name>` SHALL evaluate the machine-checkable gate conditions for exactly one change and encode the aggregate in its exit code: 0 = every applicable check passed (`GATE: PASS`), 1 = at least one check blocked (`GATE: BLOCKED (<n> item(s))`), 2 = the evaluation itself is untrustworthy (usage error, invalid or escaping name, change found nowhere, unreadable flow-state, or an untrustworthy C1 verify run). It SHALL be strictly read-only and SHALL state in its output that PASS covers mechanical checks only — human gates remain human. C6 (KB freshness) SHALL bind each touched store module to its truth doc through an index rather than a filename assumption: a truth doc MAY declare, in its header region (before the first `##`), `store-module: <name>...` (the store modules it covers; default = the doc's basename) and `source-files: <path>...` (space-separated repo-relative code paths; default = `lib/<module>.js`); a covered module with a valid `source-commit` stamp is always mechanically checked, never silently skipped.

#### Scenario: GT-01 a clean in-flight change passes
- WHEN every applicable check passes for an in-flight change
- THEN each check reports `✓`, the final line is `GATE: PASS` (with the mechanical-only caveat), and the exit code is 0

#### Scenario: GT-02 an unchecked task blocks
- WHEN the resolved change dir's tasks.md contains an unchecked `- [ ]` box (while `- [x]` and `- [X]` count as checked)
- THEN C2 reports `✗` naming the file, the final line is `GATE: BLOCKED` with the count, and the exit code is 1

#### Scenario: GT-03 the ledger blocks on open rows and reasonless rejections
- WHEN the bundle ledger at `<changeDir>/review/issues.md` contains a row whose status starts `open` (case-insensitive), or a `rejected` row with no reason text beyond the word itself
- THEN C4 blocks naming the row ID; a `rejected` row passes only when, after stripping the leading word `rejected`, the remaining text contains at least one word character (`rejected: duplicate` passes; `rejected`, `rejected:`, `rejected -` block); `advisory*`/`fixed`/`verified` rows never block

#### Scenario: GT-04 flow-state legality is enforced
- WHEN a required flow-state key (`change`, `tier`, `track`, `lineage`, `current-step`) is missing, still a `<placeholder>`, has a `current-step` outside the exact vocabulary (STEP0..STEP6, INTENT-CARD, SPIKE, EXTRACTION, DONE, ABANDONED), a `tier` outside {trivial, medium, large}, or a `change` value that does not equal `--change`
- THEN C3 blocks naming the offending key; a fully legal flow-state passes

#### Scenario: GT-05 verdict evidence is mechanical
- WHEN a review doc in the bundle (files matching `<changeDir>/review/*.md`, excluding `issues.md` and `*-raw` stems, regular files only) contains a `^VERDICT:` line but no file that is REGULAR by lstat (symlinks are not evidence) matches `<changeDir>/review/<stem>-raw.*`
- THEN C5 blocks naming the doc; adding the raw beside it flips it to `✓`; a symlink matching the doc glob blocks naming the symlink (an evidence doc must never silently drop out by file type); a `review` entry that is a symlink, escapes the change dir (realpath), or is not a directory at all blocks C4 and C5 naming the path and the defect — never read through, never crashed on — and the check works identically at both stages because the evidence travels with the dir

#### Scenario: GT-06 the binding gate is stage-aware
- WHEN the change is in-flight
- THEN C1 runs the projected verify (`verify --change` semantics, same code path incl. CAS and hygiene); WHEN the change exists only under `apriori/changes/archive/<stamp>-<name>/` THEN C1 runs plain verify against `apriori/specs/` and flow-state/tasks resolve from the archived dir; verify gaps → C1 blocked with the red/unbound/orphan counts; a verify-untrustworthy run → gate exit 2 carrying verify's errors

#### Scenario: GT-07 resolution is validated and deterministic
- WHEN `--change` fails bare-kebab-case validation or the resolved dir escapes its root (realpath containment)
- THEN gate exits 2 before reading anything; WHEN the change is found in neither location THEN gate exits 2 naming both searched paths; WHEN several archived dirs match `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T\d{4}-<name>$/ THEN the lexicographically last basename is used

#### Scenario: GT-08 a missing or mismatched flow-state fails closed
- WHEN the resolved change dir has no readable flow-state.md
- THEN gate exits 2 (tier-aware checks are impossible); a readable flow-state whose `change` key mismatches `--change` is C3-blocked, not exit 2

#### Scenario: GT-09 trivial tier is not asked for artifacts it never produces
- WHEN flow-state declares `tier: trivial` and tasks.md or the bundle ledger `<changeDir>/review/issues.md` is absent
- THEN C2/C4 report `–` (not applicable) instead of blocking; on medium/large the same absences block naming the exact bundle path

#### Scenario: GT-10 KB freshness degrades honestly through the truth index
- WHEN a touched module `<m>` (first path segment of the change's delta-spec suffixes) resolves through the truth index to a truth doc carrying a canonical `source-commit` stamp (a fence-outside line-start `source-commit: <ref>`), whose resolved `source-files` are all verifiable, and git reports commits in `<ref>..HEAD -- <source-files...>`
- THEN C6 blocks with the commit count; an up-to-date stamp passes; a module with no truth doc at all yields `–` (KB is optional — a genuine absence, not a silent skip); a missing git or a non-zero git exit yields `–` with the reason — an infra failure never fabricates a block

#### Scenario: GT-18 the truth index binds by declaration, not filename
- WHEN a store module's truth doc lives under a DIFFERENT basename (e.g. `apriori/truth/poll.md` covering store module `quick-poll`) and declares `store-module: quick-poll`
- THEN C6 finds it through the index and mechanically checks it (blocking when its `source-commit` is stale) — never reporting "no truth doc" for a module that a declaration covers; and two truth docs declaring the same module is a C6 block naming both files

#### Scenario: GT-19 an explicit source-files declaration is a complete promise
- WHEN a truth doc declares `source-files: src/server.js src/model.js` (code outside `lib/`) with a stale stamp
- THEN C6 runs git over exactly those paths (a declared DIRECTORY path is valid — git logs the whole tree) and blocks on commits since the stamp; and if an EXPLICIT `source-files` carries any token that is missing, malformed, a dangling or resolving symlink, or escapes the repo (realpath), C6 BLOCKS naming that token — partial-missing, partial-symlink, all-symlink, and one bad token beside good ones alike — because a declaration is a complete promise, while a field-less truth doc whose default `lib/<module>.js` is absent stays a `–` note (existing-layout compatibility)

#### Scenario: GT-20 malformed source-commit stamps are diagnosed, not silently skipped
- WHEN a truth doc's `source-commit` appears only in a non-canonical form — a blockquote `> \`source-commit: …\``, an HTML comment `<!-- source-commit: … -->`, an indented line, or backtick-wrapped — with no fence-outside bare line-start form
- THEN C6 reports a `–` note pointing at the required format (a fence-outside line-start `source-commit: <ref>`) rather than a vague "has no source-commit"; a `source-commit:` occurring only inside a code fence is a documentation example and raises no diagnostic

#### Scenario: GT-21 field-less truth docs behave exactly as before
- WHEN a truth doc carries neither `store-module` nor `source-files` (this repo's own docs)
- THEN C6 falls back to module = basename and source-files = `lib/<module>.js`, producing the byte-identical `{status, detail}` it produced before this change for that module — the index and declarations add coverage for non-default layouts without altering any default-layout result

#### Scenario: GT-11 --json is pure JSON in every outcome class
- WHEN `gate --change <name> --json` runs — PASS, BLOCKED, or any exit-2 class (usage error, invalid name, not found, unreadable flow-state, untrustworthy verify)
- THEN stdout parses as JSON shaped `{ change, stage: "in-flight"|"archived"|null, checks:[{id,status,detail}], result: "PASS"|"BLOCKED"|"ERROR", blocked, errors }` — `stage: null` when resolution never happened, `change: null` when `--change` was missing

#### Scenario: GT-12 gate is read-only
- WHEN gate runs to any outcome against a project tree
- THEN no file in the tree is created, modified, or deleted (only the C1 test command's own side effects, which gate does not add to)

### Requirement: C4 speaks the terminal-state vocabulary
Gate C4 SHALL parse every ledger row's status against the legal vocabulary (leading token, case-insensitive): non-terminal `open` / `fixed` / `rejected + reason`; terminal `verified` / `rejected-verified + reason` / `waived + reason` / `advisory-acked`. ANY other status blocks at BOTH stages. `rejected`, `rejected-verified`, and `waived` without a word-character reason block at both stages. A `waived` row additionally requires machine-checked human evidence at both stages: the change's flow-state `gates:` block must contain the row's ID and the text `waiv` (case-insensitive) — a producer-written row alone never passes. At the ARCHIVED stage every row must be terminal: `fixed` and plain `rejected` block with a cure naming the reviewer's verify/concur duty or the human waive. In-flight, `fixed` and reasoned `rejected` pass as today. The ledger lives at `<changeDir>/review/issues.md` at both stages.

#### Scenario: GT-13 archived ledgers must be terminal
- WHEN gate resolves a change at the archived stage whose ledger carries a `fixed` row, a plain reasoned `rejected` row, or an unknown status like `done`
- THEN C4 reports BLOCKED naming each row and a cure, while an all-terminal ledger (verified / rejected-verified + reason / gates:-backed waived + reason / advisory-acked) passes

#### Scenario: GT-14 waives belong to humans, unknown states belong to nobody
- WHEN a ledger carries a `waived — reason` row without a matching flow-state gates: entry (the ID plus "waiv"), or any row whose status is outside the vocabulary, at either stage
- THEN C4 reports BLOCKED naming the row — and the same waived row passes once the gates: entry records the human decision

#### Scenario: GT-15 every archived ledger in this repo parses legal and terminal
- WHEN the corpus of archived changes (apriori/changes/archive/*) is walked and each bundle's ledger at `<archived dir>/review/issues.md` is parsed
- THEN every row is legal AND terminal under the vocabulary (skip-if-absent for packaged environments)

### Requirement: C7 denies unstamped mutation deltas unless visibly waived
Gate SHALL run a seventh check: the change's projection carrying `unstampedMutations` → `C7 BLOCKED` naming each suffix and the stamp cure. Two escapes, flag over config: `gate --no-cas` → the check reports `waived (--no-cas)`; a process-config `cas` row read through the shared structured reader with value `optional` (leading token, case-insensitive; an absent row or `required` means required) → `waived (process-config)`. A cas CONFLICT or illegal value at consultation makes C7 BLOCKED naming the config error — bad config never equals a waiver, though the `--no-cas` flag still waives explicitly. A waiver is always visible in the gate output — never a silent skip. The waiver vocabulary is shared with `archive` (which denies by default since 4.0.1); verify's projection stays warn-only. In-flight only: at the archived stage the deltas are already merged and C7 reports n/a.

#### Scenario: GT-16 C7 blocks, and waivers are loud
- WHEN gate runs on a change whose delta carries unstamped mutation ops
- THEN C7 reports BLOCKED naming the suffix and the cure; with --no-cas or the live `| cas | optional |` config row it reports the waiver by name instead of blocking (the flag also wins when the config says required), and a stamped or ADDED-only change passes C7 silently

#### Scenario: GT-17 bad cas config blocks instead of waiving
- WHEN the process-config carries a cas CONFLICT (two live rows, different values) or a fenced-only `optional` row, and gate runs on an unstamped-mutation change
- THEN C7 reports BLOCKED — naming the config conflict in the first case, and the missing waiver in the second (fenced rows grant nothing); adding `--no-cas` waives either way

===== apriori/specs/check/spec.md =====
### Requirement: check ports the v2 doc checker to JS and adds ID coverage
`apriori check` SHALL reproduce every structural check of v2's `check_docs.py` in JS with equivalent behavior, and additionally enforce that every spec scenario carries a bindable ID.

#### Scenario: CK-01 anchor and file-link checks behave as v2
- WHEN a doc has a broken `](#anchor)` or `](./file)` link
- THEN check reports it and exits non-zero, matching the Python checker's verdict

#### Scenario: CK-02 EN/CN alignment checks behave as v2
- WHEN bilingual docs are present and their heading sequences or verdict phrases diverge
- THEN check reports the misalignment (same rules as the ported checker)

#### Scenario: CK-03 verdict-phrase-table and codex-command checks behave as v2
- WHEN a verdict-line drift variant or an EN/CN codex-command mismatch is introduced
- THEN check reports it (the v2.3 checkers 6-8, ported)

#### Scenario: CK-04 every spec scenario must carry an ID (new)
- WHEN a `#### Scenario:` heading in the spec store lacks a leading id-pattern match
- THEN check reports it as unbindable and exits non-zero (a scenario with no ID can never pass verify)

#### Scenario: CK-05 no OpenSpec adapter assertions remain
- WHEN check runs against v3 docs
- THEN it enforces the single plain-files interface (no `openspec/`-adapter dual-path assertions from v2)

### Requirement: check warns on a stale scaffolded runbook without failing
`apriori check` SHALL compare the project's scaffolded `apriori/runbook.md` (when present) against the installed package's `RUNBOOK.md` and warn on divergence, without turning the warning into a failure.

#### Scenario: CK-06 stale scaffolded runbook warns, never fails
- WHEN `apriori check` runs in a project whose `apriori/runbook.md` differs byte-wise from the installed package's runbook
- THEN it prints a warning naming `apriori update`, and RESULT stays PASS if nothing else failed; a missing `apriori/runbook.md` produces no warning

#### Scenario: CK-07 consumer mode is the default; self-checks require --self
- WHEN `apriori check` runs in a consumer project
- THEN only the spec-store checks (CK-04), runbook freshness (CK-06), and the review-evidence secret tripwire (CK-10) run — a consumer legitimately using OpenSpec or shipping its own README is never failed by apriori's handbook self-checks (EN/CN pairs, verdict phrases, codex forms, no-openspec), which run only under `--self`; and a missing spec-store path is an error (exit 2, naming `apriori init` when uninitialized), never a silent PASS

### Requirement: self-mode guards the split documentation set
`apriori check --self` SHALL extend its EN/CN pair coverage to the docs/ pairs (concepts, legacy, ci, cli, troubleshooting — `_cn` suffix convention) and SHALL resolve links relative to the linking file, validating cross-file fragments.

#### Scenario: CK-08 docs pairs are guarded, one-sided pairs fail
- WHEN `check --self` runs where a docs/ pair misaligns (heading count, level, or numeric prefix), or exactly ONE side of a pair exists
- THEN it FAILs naming the pair (or the missing mirror); WHEN both sides of a pair are absent THEN that pair is skipped and older checkouts pass as before

#### Scenario: CK-09 links resolve from the linking file and fragments are validated
- WHEN a checked file links `./y.md` or `./y.md#frag`
- THEN the target resolves relative to THAT file's directory (root files unchanged); a missing target file FAILs naming the linking file; and a fragment with no heading in the target slugifying (ghSlug) to it FAILs naming both — self-mode only

### Requirement: review evidence is guarded against committed secrets
`apriori check` (consumer mode) SHALL scan every `review/` directory under `apriori/changes/*/` and `apriori/changes/archive/*/` — recursive, regular files only, symlinked entries skipped with a warn line naming them, an absent dir skipped — for exactly three literal secret formats: AWS access keys (`AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}`), GitHub tokens (`gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}`), and PEM private-key headers (`-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----`). Root discovery is itself guarded: each discovered change dir and its `review/` must realpath-resolve inside the changes root; escaping or symlinked dirs are warn-skipped like symlinked files. A hit SHALL fail the check naming the file, line number, and pattern class — never echoing the matched value — with a remedy pointer (sanitize the raw; if already pushed, rewrite history per SECURITY.md).

#### Scenario: CK-10 committed secrets in review evidence fail the check
- WHEN a file under any bundle's `review/` (active or archived, any depth) contains an AWS key, a GitHub token, or a PEM private-key header
- THEN `check` FAILs naming the file, line and pattern class without echoing the secret, and the message points at the remedy; clean bundles pass; a symlinked entry or an escaping review/ dir is skipped with a warn naming it; a project with no bundles skips the check entirely

### Requirement: CK-11 keeps the runbook version aligned with the CLI major
`apriori check --self` SHALL assert that RUNBOOK.md — the canonical packaged runbook — carries exactly one header-blockquote entry of the form `` > `runbook-version: X.Y` `` whose major (`X`) equals `package.json`'s version major; RUNBOOK_cn.md, when present, SHALL likewise carry exactly one such entry whose major also equals the package major (each edition equals the package major, not merely each other). A missing entry, more than one, or a malformed value FAILs (self-mode only) naming the file and the failure reason; a major mismatch FAILs naming the file, the runbook major, and the package major. Occurrences of `runbook-version:` in body text or code fences are never matched. Consumer `apriori check` (no `--self`) never runs CK-11.

#### Scenario: CK-11 the runbook major tracks the CLI major
- WHEN `apriori check --self` runs where RUNBOOK.md and RUNBOOK_cn.md declare `runbook-version: 4.0` and package.json is on a 4.x version
- THEN CK-11 passes; flipping either edition's header to a `3.0` major FAILs (self-mode) naming the file, the runbook major, and the package major; and a consumer `apriori check` without `--self` never raises CK-11

#### Scenario: CK-12 malformed, missing, duplicate, and body occurrences
- WHEN a runbook edition has no `runbook-version` blockquote entry, has two of them, carries a malformed value (`runbook-version: vier`), or mentions `runbook-version:` only in body text or inside a code fence
- THEN the missing/duplicate/malformed cases FAIL (self-mode) naming the file and the reason, while the body-text and code-fence occurrences are never matched (they alone do not satisfy or fail the check — a real header entry is still required)

===== apriori/specs/doctor/spec.md =====
### Requirement: doctor diagnoses the project-apriori seam
`apriori doctor` SHALL run eight read-only checks (D1 Node floor (≥22) · D2 init scaffold · D3 runbook freshness · D4 tool pointers · D5 TAP probe · D6 store health · D7 changes overview · D8 legacy 3.x layout roots), each reporting ok/finding/not-applicable with a fix hint on findings, and SHALL encode the aggregate in its exit code: 0 = zero findings (`DOCTOR: HEALTHY`), 1 = ≥1 finding (`DOCTOR: <n> finding(s)`), 2 = unusable (Node below floor, `apriori/` missing entirely, positional-arg usage error) — printing whatever checks already ran. Doctor never repairs and never writes; its ONLY side effect is D5 executing the project's test command once, removed by `--no-run`.

#### Scenario: DR-01 a healthy initialized project reports HEALTHY
- WHEN every applicable check passes on a freshly initialized project with a valid TAP test command
- THEN each check reports ok or not-applicable, the final line is `DOCTOR: HEALTHY`, and the exit code is 0

#### Scenario: DR-02 an uninitialized project is unusable, with guidance
- WHEN `apriori/` does not exist under the target root
- THEN doctor exits 2, the message names `apriori init`, and `--json` reports `result: "UNUSABLE"` with the already-run checks (D1) listed

#### Scenario: DR-03 init-scaffold gaps are findings with their fixer
- WHEN `apriori/runbook.md`, `apriori/specs/`, `apriori/.gitignore` (with its `tmp/` line), or the `apriori/tmp/` dir is missing
- THEN D2 emits ONE check entry PER GAP (each with id `D2`, status finding, naming the file and its fixer `apriori init` / `apriori update`) — the aggregate `findings` count equals the number of finding-status entries; a missing `apriori/process-config.md` is not-applicable (optional, defaults apply), never a finding

#### Scenario: DR-04 runbook freshness points at update, and never contradicts D2
- WHEN the runbook copy differs by any byte from the packaged RUNBOOK.md
- THEN D3 is a finding naming `apriori update`; WHEN the copy is absent THEN D3 is not-applicable ("see D2") — absence is D2's finding alone

#### Scenario: DR-05 detected tools must keep their pointers
- WHEN a tool that `detectTools` finds has a missing rules file, a rules file without the `apriori/runbook.md` pointer, or (command-level tools) a missing command file
- THEN D4 reports a finding per tool naming the file and `apriori init`; a project with no known tool markers reports not-applicable

#### Scenario: DR-06 the TAP probe classifies every plumbing edge
- WHEN the test command (from `--test-cmd` or the config row) runs
- THEN D5 classifies: spawn error, signal kill, `Bail out!`, empty output, zero parsed TAP without a version/plan line (naming `--test-reporter=tap`), a TAP version or non-`1..0` plan with zero result lines ("truncated or malformed"), and a non-zero exit unexplained by parsed TAP failures — each a finding; parsed TAP whose failures explain any non-zero exit is ok (counts as info — test failures are NOT doctor findings), and an exactly-`1..0` plan with exit status 0 is ok ("empty suite") — a `1..0` plan with a NON-ZERO exit is the unexplained-exit finding, classified first

#### Scenario: DR-07 the probe is skippable and degrades honestly
- WHEN no test command is configured anywhere, or `--no-run` is given
- THEN D5 reports not-applicable with the reason (the `apriori init --test-cmd` hint, or "probe skipped") — never a finding

#### Scenario: DR-08 store health flags unbindable and ambiguous scenarios
- WHEN `apriori/specs/` contains scenarios without a leading default-pattern ID, or the same ID in more than one scenario
- THEN D6 reports findings listing them (detail names the default ID pattern); an empty store or zero scenario files is not-applicable ("normal for a new project"); a missing specs dir is not-applicable here ("see D2")

#### Scenario: DR-09 changes overview validates flow-states and surfaces pending gates
- WHEN an active change dir has no readable flow-state.md, or `parseFlowState` yields an empty `change`, or its `change` mismatches the dir name
- THEN D7 reports a finding naming the dir and the failed clause; healthy active changes are info lines; archived candidates (basenames matching `<YYYY-MM-DDThhmm>-<name>`, directories only) are read ONLY when they satisfy realpath containment under `apriori/changes/archive/` — an escaping symlinked entry is skipped with an info note, never read; contained not-DONE/ABANDONED archived changes are listed as info ("gate ④ possibly pending") — never a finding

#### Scenario: DR-10 output is machine-consumable in every class
- WHEN `apriori doctor --json` runs — HEALTHY, FINDINGS, or UNUSABLE (incl. the positional-arg usage error)
- THEN stdout parses as JSON shaped `{ result, findings, checks: [{id, status: "ok"|"finding"|"n/a", detail, fix?}], errors }`

#### Scenario: DR-11 doctor is read-only and rejects stray arguments
- WHEN doctor runs with `--no-run` against any project tree
- THEN no file is created, modified, or deleted; WHEN any positional argument is given THEN doctor exits 2 with usage (pure JSON under `--json`)

#### Scenario: DR-12 the Node floor is enforced testably
- WHEN the detected Node major version (injectable for tests) is below 22
- THEN D1 is a finding entry and doctor exits 2 (`result: "UNUSABLE"`), with D1 listed in the checks; at or above 22 → ok

### Requirement: doctor detects legacy 3.x layout roots
Doctor SHALL check (D8) for the five pre-4.0 scattered roots — top-level `requirement/`, top-level `spike/`, `apriori/review/`, `apriori/design/`, `apriori/explore/` — using existence-level probes only (lstat/exists, never following or reading through); any hit is a finding listing every root found, with a fix pointing at the MIGRATING.md 4.0 section. A clean 4.0 project reports D8 ok; the check performs zero writes.

#### Scenario: DR-13 mixed 3.x layouts are named, clean ones pass
- WHEN a project carries any of the five legacy roots (a directory or a symlink at that path both count), and another project carries none
- THEN doctor reports a D8 finding naming exactly the roots present with a fix pointing at the migration guidance, and the clean project's D8 is ok — with no file content read and nothing written

### Requirement: the probe speaks the same TAP as verify
`classifyProbe` SHALL consume the shared version-aware lexer and judge by the D5 matrix: probe TAP containing failures (any shape, unattributed included) is D5 **ok** with a detail noting the failures are verify's business and the TAP channel itself is healthy; an unsupported TAP version is D5 **finding** naming the version and the supported matrix; a stdout-empty probe whose stderr carries TAP-shaped output is D5 **finding** with a `2>&1` fix; every other classification and doctor's exit taxonomy stay unchanged.

#### Scenario: DR-14 the D5 matrix follows the lexer
- WHEN probes emit: a failing `not ok` with exit 0; a `TAP version 99` stream; TAP on stderr with empty stdout; and a healthy TAP-13 stream
- THEN D5 reports ok (failures noted as verify's business), finding (version named), finding (`2>&1` fix), and ok respectively — with doctor's exit taxonomy unchanged

### Requirement: doctor reads config through the structured reader
Doctor's config consumption (the D5 probe's test-cmd fallback) SHALL use the shared structured reader; a `test-cmd` CONFLICT is a D5 finding naming the config problem rather than a probe run over an arbitrary row.

#### Scenario: DR-15 doctor refuses to probe on conflicted config
- WHEN doctor runs (no explicit test command) in a project whose process-config carries two live conflicting `test-cmd` rows
- THEN D5 is a finding naming the config conflict and no probe command executes

===== apriori/truth/gate.md =====
# TRUTH — gate (`lib/gate.js`, CLI subcommand `gate`)

## Contract (code-is-truth)

source-commit: 83fd13e

**Responsibility.** Aggregate the MECHANICAL exit conditions for one change into one exit code: 0 PASS · 1 BLOCKED · 2 evaluation untrustworthy. Strictly read-only. PASS covers machine checks only — human gates remain human (stated in output).

**Public interface.**
- `runGate({cwd, change, testCmd})` → `{ code, stage: 'in-flight'|'archived'|null, checks:[{id,status:'pass'|'blocked'|'n/a',detail}], result:'PASS'|'BLOCKED'|'ERROR', blocked, errors, change }`.
- `resolveChange(cwd, name)` → `{stage, dir}` or `{error}` — in-flight `apriori/changes/<name>` first (realpath containment vs changes dir); else archived: basenames matching `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T\d{4}-<name>$/` under `changes/archive/` that stat as DIRECTORIES, sorted, last; containment vs the ARCHIVE root (a symlink pointing elsewhere under changes/ is an escape).
- `cli(argv)`: `--change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--cwd <dir>] [--json / --no-cas (C7 waiver, visible in the check detail)]` via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md); testCmd falls back to `configTestCmd(cwd)`; neither → exit 2. `--json` = pure JSON in EVERY outcome class incl. parse errors (the jsonError seam).

**The seven checks.** C1 binding (in-flight → `verify({change})` projected; archived → `verify({specs:[apriori/specs]})`; verify infra errors → gate exit 2) · C2 tasks.md zero `- [ ]` (`[x]`/`[X]` checked; absent → n/a on trivial, blocked otherwise) · C3 flow-state legality (required keys change/tier/track/lineage/current-step; placeholder = contains `<`/`>`; exact step/tier enums; change must equal --change; file missing → exit 2) · C4 ledger at `<dir>/review/issues.md` (the resolved change dir's bundle) BOTH stages, speaking the terminal-state vocabulary since ledger-states (3.3.x): statuses classified by `classifyStatus` (exported; leading token case-insensitive, `rejected-verified` alternated before `rejected`) — unknown statuses block at both stages; `open` blocks; `rejected`/`rejected-verified`/`waived` need a word character after the token; a `waived` row needs same-entry human evidence in the change's flow-state `gates:` block (one entry carrying the row ID as an exact token — LS-1 never matches inside LS-10 — plus /waiv/i); at the ARCHIVED stage every row must be terminal (`verified`/`rejected-verified`/`waived`/`advisory-acked`) — `fixed` and plain `rejected` block with reviewer-verify/concur-or-human-waive cures; in-flight `fixed` and reasoned `rejected` pass (absent ledger → n/a on trivial) · C5 verdict evidence (docs `<dir>/review/*.md`, minus `issues.md` and raw stems, regular files only — symlinked doc blocks; `/^VERDICT:/m` doc needs a REGULAR (lstat-first) `<dir>/review/<stem>-raw.*` beside it). Shared C4/C5 evidence-root guard `reviewDirDefect(dir)`: `<dir>/review` is lstat-ed FIRST (only ENOENT is absence — a dangling symlink is a defect); symlink / non-dir / escape (containsReal) blocks BOTH C4 and C5 naming the defect · C6 KB freshness (modules = first segments of delta-spec suffixes; precondition ladder → n/a with reason; `git -C <cwd> log --oneline <stamp>..HEAD -- lib/<m>.js`: clean pass / commits block / non-zero git exit n/a). · C7 CAS default-deny (in-flight: the projection's `unstampedMutations` block naming each suffix + the stamp cure; waivers are VISIBLE — `--no-cas` flag or a `| cas | optional |` process-config row (flag wins, `configCas` leading value token case-insensitive); archived → n/a, deltas already merged; neither waiver touches verify/archive behavior)

**Pitfalls.**
- C1 runs the project's full test command — gate's runtime is dominated by it.
- C6's module→code mapping is the `lib/<m>.js` convention; anything off-convention degrades to n/a (never a false block).
- GT-12's read-only test fingerprints path+size+mtime — same-size rewrites with restored mtime would evade it; static inspection is the actual guarantee (gate performs zero writes).
- Requires `./status`, `./spec-runner`, `./archive-merge`; nothing requires gate (leaf module).

## Decisions (doc-is-truth)

- D-GT-1 (active): gate judges the MECHANICAL face only; its PASS never substitutes for protected human gates — the output must say so. Ratified in gate-command (2026-07-11).
- D-GT-2 (active): infra failures (git absent, bad stamp commit, unmappable module) yield n/a with a reason, never a fabricated block — false blocks train humans to ignore the gate.
- D-GT-3 (amended by change-bundle, 2026-07-12): the raw-evidence naming rule is normative: a review doc with stem S archives its raw as `<dir>/review/S-raw.*` inside its change bundle; pre-4.0 archives were migrated to this shape.
- D-GT-4 (active): C4/C5 read the bundle evidence root `<dir>/review/` and work identically in-flight and archived because the evidence travels with the dir; the root itself is guarded lstat-first — dangling symlinks are defects, never absence. Ratified in change-bundle (2026-07-12).

===== apriori/truth/spec-runner.md =====
# TRUTH — spec-runner (`lib/spec-runner.js`, CLI subcommand `verify`)

## Contract (code-is-truth)

source-commit: d7d65d3

**Responsibility.** Bind spec scenario IDs to a TAP-emitting test run; report per-scenario red/green; act as STEP5's deterministic gate. Fail-closed: a broken or vacuous run is never GREEN. 3.1: `--change <name>` verifies against the in-memory PROJECTED store (archive's merge() applied to the living store) — the mid-change form.

**Public interface (module exports).**
- `leadId(text, idRe)` → leading ID or null. Word-boundary strict: `XX-01b`, `XX-01_x` are NOT `XX-01`.
- `stripFences(text)` → text with ``` fenced blocks removed (fenced scenarios are documentation, not spec).
- `mdFiles` is internal; `collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe)` → `{ byId, unidentified:[[file,title]], files, missingTargets, duplicates:[{id,files}] }`. A target may be a dir (recursed for `*.md`, sorted) or a single file (accepted as-is, ANY extension); dedupes overlapping targets. Scenario discovery matches exact h4 lines `#### Scenario: …` only. Scenarios inside DEPRECATED requirement blocks (heading carries `_deprecated (superseded by …)_`) are excluded in EVERY form — not demanded, not listed; their lingering tests turn ORPHAN.
- `collectScenariosFromTexts(Map<label,text>, idRe)` — same scan over in-memory texts (the projection path); labels are store-relative suffixes.
- `parseTap(out, idRe)` → `{ results: Map<id,{pass,fail,skip}>, untagged:[], untaggedFails, bailout, plans:number[], points, dupNumbers:number[] }`. Only lines shaped exactly `ok|not ok <n> - <name>` BIND — a TAP result line without the ` - ` separator produces no binding. Since tap-plan (3.3.x) a per-line pre-pass (one trailing `\r` stripped) additionally collects: `plans` = every top-level `1..N` (trailing `# SKIP`/`# TODO` directive legal, N still parses); `points` = count of top-level result tokens `/^(?:ok|not ok)(?:\s|$)/` (bare/unnumbered points count; `ok:`-prefixed and indented lines never do); `dupNumbers` = complete numeric point tokens (`ok 1abc` stays unnumbered) seen more than once, numerically. `# SKIP`/`# TODO` counts as skip (proves nothing); untagged `not ok` without directive increments `untaggedFails`; `Bail out!` line captured.
- `zeroTapParsed(out, results, untagged)` → true when output exists but no TAP line parsed AND no `TAP version`/`1..N` plan line (plan line = TAP reporter with an empty suite, not a reporter problem).
- `runTestCommand(cmd, cwd)` → `{ out (stdout+\n+stderr), status (null if signal-killed), signal, error }` via `spawnSync(shell:true)`.
- `evaluate(byId, unidentified, results)` → `{ boundGreen, boundRed, unbound, orphan, unidentified, clean }`. A scenario whose only results are skips is UNBOUND.
- `verify(opts {specs:[], change, testCmd, idPattern, cwd})` — with `change`: builds the projection via archive-merge's discoverDeltas/buildProjection (roots `<cwd>/apriori/{changes,specs}`); ANY projection failure (validation/hygiene/CAS/merge-conflict) → run with `errors` set and NO test executed; success → normal pipeline over projected texts; run gains `projection: {change, modules, conflicts}`. Without `change`: unchanged. → run object `{ verdict, results, fileCount, failCount, scenarioCount, missingTargets, duplicates, bailout, noTap, exec{status,signal,error}, errors }`. `failCount` = tagged fails + `untaggedFails`.
- `infraErrors(run)` → string[] (empty = trustworthy). Triggers: missing spec target · zero spec files · zero scenarios · spawn error · signal kill · bailout · zero-TAP-parsed · plan trio (defensive defaults keep legacy run shapes valid: multiple top-level plans → error naming the cure; exactly one plan and plan ≠ points → truncated/garbled; exactly one plan and duplicate point numbers) · non-zero exit with `failCount === 0` (TAP picture doesn't explain the exit). Gate surfaces any of these as ERROR exit 2 (`verify: <msg>`), outranking a C1 binding block; doctor's probe classification intentionally does not consume infraErrors.
- `formatReport(...)` → human report; RESULT line: `ERROR — run not trustworthy (see stderr)` / `GREEN — spec is the test suite` / `GAPS`. Duplicates block GREEN.
- `verifyJson(run)` → `{ clean, result: GREEN|GAPS|ERROR, errors, specFiles, exec, duplicates, boundGreen[], boundRed[], unbound[], orphan[], unidentified[] }`; `--change` runs add exactly one field `projection: {change, modules, conflicts}` — never present otherwise. Since cas-enforcement (3.3.x): `--change` runs carry `projection.unstampedMutations` (suffixes, `[]` when none) + `projection.notes`; the cli prints one stderr warning per unstamped mutation delta — verdict untouched (WARN grade).
- `configTestCmd(cwd)` → test-cmd from `apriori/process-config.md`'s `| test-cmd | … |` row, or null.
- `cli(argv)` → exit code via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md): --help exits 0; unknown flags/stray positionals exit 2. `--specs <dir...>` OR `--change <name>` (mutually exclusive) `--test-cmd "<cmd>" [--id-pattern] [--cwd] [--json]`; test-cmd falls back to config row. Exit: 0 GREEN · 1 gaps/duplicates · 2 infra/usage error, incl. all projection failure classes.
- `DEFAULT_ID` = `[A-Z]+-\d+`.

**Data flow.** spec targets → collectScenarios → run test cmd once → parseTap → evaluate → infraErrors → report/JSON. One test-command invocation per verify run; no state persists between runs (stateless, reads FS + spawns only).

**Three moments.** No external shared state. FS reads only; the spawned test command is the only side effect.

**Pitfalls.**
- `--specs` consumes following args until the next `--` flag — a path starting with `--` is impossible.
- A direct-file `--specs` target is read whatever its extension; only directory recursion filters to `.md`.
- `exec.status` is null when signal-killed; the non-zero-exit rule explicitly guards `status !== null`.
- Requires `./archive-merge` (one-way: archive-merge never requires spec-runner).

## Decisions (doc-is-truth)

- D-SR-1 (active): fail-closed is a hard product stance — exit taxonomy 0/1/2; a vacuous or untrustworthy run must never exit 0. Ratified with 3.0.1 (owner + external review 2026-07-10).
- D-SR-2 (active): skipped tests prove nothing — a scenario with only skips is UNBOUND, not green.
- D-SR-3 (active): duplicate scenario IDs are a binding ambiguity → GAPS (exit 1), not a warning.
- D-SR-4 (active): the binding gate speaks TAP only; non-TAP layers (e.g. Playwright E2E) sit above the gate as additional exit conditions, never replace it.
- D-SR-5 (active): deprecated requirement blocks are excluded from scenario collection in ALL verify forms — post-REMOVED stores must not demand tests for removed behavior forever; lingering tests surface as ORPHAN. Ratified in change-projection (2026-07-10, STEP0 REQ-2).
- D-SR-6 (active): mid-change, the STEP5 gate is `verify --change` (projection); plain `--specs` verify is the post-archive/store-only form. Ratified in change-projection.
- D-SR-x (active): any top-level non-directive `not ok` blocks GREEN whatever its shape — unattributed failures are GAPS (exit 1) even on exit 0, infra ERRORs keep precedence, and a non-zero exec status never exits 0. Ratified in unattributed-fail (2026-07-12, GPT-5.6 review P0-1).
- D-SR-y (active): TAP is parsed by a version-aware line lexer over stdout only — closed version matrix {12,13,14,absent}, escape-aware directive splitting, opaque fail-closed YAML blocks, pragma-only ignoring; stderr is a reported diagnostics channel, never parsed. Ratified in tap-contract (2026-07-12, GPT-5.6 fourth review).

===== apriori/truth/check.md =====
# TRUTH — check (`lib/check.js`, CLI subcommand `check`)

## Contract (code-is-truth)

source-commit: f6a0a13

**Responsibility.** Structural consistency checks. Two modes: CONSUMER (default) — CK-04 (bindable scenario IDs; missing store path → exit 2) + CK-06 (runbook freshness, warn-only) + CK-10 (review-evidence secret tripwire: three literal patterns over EVERY bundle's `review/` under `apriori/changes/` and `apriori/changes/archive/`; discovery is lstat-guarded — symlinked change dirs and symlinked/escaping `review/` entries (dangling included) are warn-skipped by name, never followed; recursive, regular files, per-file symlink warn-skip, absence skips silently; `checkReviewSecrets` exported). SELF (`--self`) — additionally the apriori repo's handbook checks (anchors/file links resolved from the LINKING file's dir with cross-file fragment validation (CK-09), EN/CN heading alignment over the ROOT pairs AND the five docs/ pairs — a one-sided pair FAILS, both-absent skips (CK-08) — verdict-phrase table + drift variants, codex command EN/CN parity + known-good resume forms, no-OpenSpec residue).

**Public interface (surfaces others may consume).**
- `checkScenarioIds(specText, name, idPattern)` → failure strings (fenced scenarios exempt).
- `checkRunbookFreshness(root, packagedPath = <pkg>/RUNBOOK.md)` → [] or one warn string (byte comparison of apriori/runbook.md vs the packaged runbook); returns [] when EITHER file is missing — absence is not warned.
- `cli(argv)`: `[--specs <dir>] [--self]` via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md — help 0 / unknown 2); exit 0 PASS · 1 FAIL(n) · 2 missing store path — the store-path requirement applies in `--self` mode too.
- Various handbook checkers (checkLinks, checkHeadingAlignment, checkVerdictPhrases, codexCandidates, checkCodexCommands, checkCodexKnownForms, checkNoOpenspec) — self-mode only.

**Pitfalls.**
- CK-06 is warn-only by design — a stale runbook never fails CI.
- CK-04 counts scenarios inside DEPRECATED requirement blocks too (check has no deprecated-block rule; verify does — a known check-vs-verify divergence on post-REMOVED stores, flagged in change-projection's gap report R2, accepted out of scope there).

## Decisions (doc-is-truth)

- D-CK-1 (active): consumer/self split — a consumer legitimately using OpenSpec or shipping its own README must never be failed by apriori's self-checks. Ratified 3.0.1.
- D-CK-2 (active): CK-10 scans bundle evidence roots only (`apriori/changes/*/review/`, active and archived) with lstat-guarded discovery — a symlinked change dir or review/ entry is warn-skipped by name, never followed. Ratified in change-bundle (2026-07-12).

===== apriori/truth/doctor.md =====
# TRUTH — doctor (`lib/doctor.js`, CLI subcommand `doctor`)

## Contract (code-is-truth)

source-commit: bfadb12

**Responsibility.** Diagnose the project↔apriori seam with seven checks; one exit code: 0 HEALTHY · 1 FINDINGS · 2 UNUSABLE (Node < 22, `apriori/` missing/not-a-dir, positional-arg usage error — each still listing the checks that ran). Never repairs, never writes; the ONLY side effect is D5 running the test command once (`--no-run` removes it). Findings carry a `fix` naming the repair command.

**Public interface.**
- `runDoctor({cwd, testCmd, noRun, nodeVersion})` → `{ code, result: 'HEALTHY'|'FINDINGS'|'UNUSABLE', findings, checks: [{id, status: 'ok'|'finding'|'n/a', detail, fix?}], errors }`. `checks` may carry MULTIPLE entries per D-id (D2 one per gap, D4 one per broken tool); `findings` = count of finding-status entries. `nodeVersion` is the DR-12 test seam.
- `classifyProbe(run)` — D5's classifier over `runTestCommand`'s shape `{out, status, signal, error}`; ladder: spawn error → signal → bailout → empty output → zeroTapParsed (non-TAP) → unexplained non-zero exit (BEFORE any ok branch — a non-zero `1..0` lands here) → zero parsed results (`1..0` ok, else truncated/malformed) → ok with p/f counts (test failures are NOT findings).
- `cli(argv)`: `[--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--no-run] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]` via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md); any positional or unknown flag → exit 2 (pure JSON under `--json` via the jsonError seam); `--json` shape `{result, findings, checks, errors}` in every class.

**The seven checks.** D1 Node ≥ 22 (injectable) · D2 scaffold with TYPE-HONEST probes (isDir/isFile/readIfFile — a file impersonating specs/ or tmp/, a dir at runbook.md/.gitignore, unreadable entries → findings, never crashes; missing process-config = n/a note) · D3 freshness via `checkRunbookFreshness`, gated on `isFile(runbook)` (absence/wrong type → n/a "see D2") · D4 pointers for `detectTools` hits only (rules file regular+contains `apriori/runbook.md`; command-level file exists) · D5 probe (above) · D6 store via `collectScenarios` default pattern (missing dir → n/a "see D2"; empty → n/a; unidentified/dupes → findings) · D7 active flow-states (exists + per-file `containsReal` before ANY read — a symlinked flow-state is diagnosed, never read + non-empty `change` + matches dir) + archived stamp-dirs (directories only, `containsReal(archRoot,…)` before ANY read; escaping symlink → skip note) with not-DONE/ABANDONED surfaced as "gate ④ possibly pending" info.

**Pitfalls.**
- Requires ./init, ./check, ./spec-runner, ./status, ./archive-merge (leaf module; nothing requires doctor).
- D5's runtime = the project's test command runtime.
- On the apriori source repo itself, doctor honestly reports FINDINGS (no runbook copy — the repo is not an initialized consumer); that is correct, not a bug.

## Decisions (doc-is-truth)

- D-DR-1 (active): test failures are NOT doctor findings — D5 probes plumbing (does the command speak TAP), not correctness; red tests are `verify`'s business. Ratified in doctor-command (2026-07-11).
- D-DR-2 (active): gate-④-pending archived changes are information, never findings — that state is the §4 archive sequencing working as designed.
- D-DR-3 (active): doctor diagnoses and points at the fixer; it never repairs — repair actions belong to `init`/`update`/the human.
- D-DR-4 (active): wrong-type scaffold entries (file-as-dir etc.) are findings, never false passes or crashes — malformed scaffolds are doctor's core case.

===== apriori/truth/args.md =====
# TRUTH — args (`lib/args.js`, shared by every subcommand)

## Contract (code-is-truth)

source-commit: 20bee2b

**Responsibility.** The one strict argv parser (CL-11..17). Nothing is silently ignored: `--help`/`-h` anywhere (alias-resolved, pre-scanned BEFORE any validation) → `{ok:false, code:0}`; unknown dash-tokens / missing values / empty multi sets / arity violations → `{ok:false, code:2, message}`.

**Public interface.**
- `parseStrict(argv, spec)` — spec `{sub, usage, flags:{'--name':'value'|'multi'|'flag'}, positionals:0|1, aliases}`. `value` consumes EXACTLY the next token verbatim (only absence errors; repeats last-write-wins); `multi` consumes until the next dash token (empty occurrence errors; repeats accumulate); `flag` idempotent.
- `withStrict(argv, spec, body)` — help → usage to stdout, 0; parse error → message+usage to stderr, 2 — UNLESS `spec.jsonError` is set and argv contains `--json` (gate GT-11 / doctor DR-10 pure-JSON contracts), then jsonError(message) to stdout, 2; ok → `body(flags, positionals)`.

**Pitfalls.**
- The three declared 3.3-line behavior changes: `new` extras error (was silent), `stamp --foo` is an unknown flag (was its positional), multi stops at single-dash tokens (was `--`-only).
- init keeps its interactive door: empty flags parse ok, the TTY decision lives in init.cli (CL-17).

## Decisions (doc-is-truth)

- D-AR-1 (active): silent argv ignore is fail-open and banned — a typo must never let a gate command act on the wrong target while exiting green. Ratified in cli-strictness (2026-07-11).

===== lib/config.js =====
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * process-config parses as structure, never as full-text regex (config-contract).
     4	 * One shared reader for every consumer: fenced code blocks and HTML comments are
     5	 * non-content (unterminated blocks make the rest of the file inert — naturally
     6	 * fail-closed for waiver keys); a config row is any |-leading table row, first
     7	 * cell key, second cell value, extra columns ignored; same-key same-value
     8	 * duplicates are tolerated, different values are a CONFLICT problem. Problems
     9	 * surface only when the key is actually consumed.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	
    14	// parse the raw text → { values: Map<key,value>, conflicts: Set<key> }
    15	function parseConfig(text) {
    16	  const values = new Map();
    17	  const conflicts = new Set();
    18	  let fence = false, comment = false;
    19	  for (const raw of (text || '').split('\n')) {
    20	    const line = raw.endsWith('\r') ? raw.slice(0, -1) : raw;
    21	    let t = line;
    22	    if (comment) {
    23	      const close = t.indexOf('-->');
    24	      if (close < 0) continue;
    25	      comment = false;
    26	      t = t.slice(close + 3);
    27	    }
    28	    if (fence) { if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) fence = false; continue; }
    29	    if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) { fence = true; continue; }
    30	    // strip inline comment SPANS — content before the opener stays live
    31	    while (t.includes('<!--')) {
    32	      const open = t.indexOf('<!--');
    33	      const close = t.indexOf('-->', open + 4);
    34	      if (close < 0) { t = t.slice(0, open); comment = true; break; }
    35	      t = t.slice(0, open) + t.slice(close + 3);
    36	    }
    37	    if (!t.trimStart().startsWith('|')) continue;
    38	    const cells = t.split('|').map((c) => c.trim());
    39	    // cells[0] is the empty prefix before the leading pipe
    40	    const key = cells[1], value = cells[2];
    41	    if (!key || value === undefined || value === '') continue;
    42	    if (key.toLowerCase() === 'key' || /^-+$/.test(key)) continue;      // header / separator
    43	    if (/^-+$/.test(value)) continue;
    44	    if (values.has(key) && values.get(key) !== value) { conflicts.add(key); continue; }
    45	    values.set(key, value);
    46	  }
    47	  return { values, conflicts };
    48	}
    49	
    50	function readConfig(cwd) {
    51	  const p = path.join(cwd || '.', 'apriori', 'process-config.md');
    52	  if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return { values: new Map(), conflicts: new Set() };
    53	  return parseConfig(fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8'));
    54	}
    55	
    56	// one key → { value: string|null, problem: string|null } (problem only when consumed)
    57	function getConfig(cwd, key) {
    58	  const { values, conflicts } = readConfig(cwd);
    59	  if (conflicts.has(key)) return { value: null, problem: `process-config carries conflicting '${key}' rows — resolve the conflict (one live row per key)` };
    60	  return { value: values.has(key) ? values.get(key) : null, problem: null };
    61	}
    62	
    63	module.exports = { parseConfig, readConfig, getConfig };

===== spec-runner 1-110 =====
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * spec-runner — bind spec scenario IDs to test runs, report per-scenario red/green.
     4	 * VISION endgame, weak form: "spec IS the test suite". Zero deps — pure Node stdlib.
     5	 * Coupling surface is tiny and language-agnostic: markdown scenarios + a TAP-emitting test command.
     6	 *
     7	 * FAIL-CLOSED: a deterministic gate must refuse to say GREEN when its inputs are missing,
     8	 * its instrument misbehaved, or its evidence is ambiguous. Vacuous success is a bug.
     9	 * Exit codes: 0 = GREEN · 1 = spec/test gaps · 2 = invocation or infrastructure error.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    14	// merge-family helpers shared with archive (3.1 projection); archive-merge never requires spec-runner
    15	const am = require('./archive-merge');
    16	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    17	
    18	const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+-\\d+';
    19	const SCENARIO_RE = /^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm;
    20	// TAP result line, directive split off separately (# SKIP / # TODO)
    21	const TAP_RE = /^(ok|not ok)\s+\d+\s+-\s+(.*)$/gm;
    22	
    23	// Leading scenario/test ID. The match must end at a word boundary: `XX-01b` is NOT `XX-01` —
    24	// silently truncating would merge two different scenarios into one binding.
    25	function leadId(text, idRe) {
    26	  const m = text.trim().match(idRe);
    27	  if (!m || m.index !== 0) return null;
    28	  const next = text.trim()[m[0].length];
    29	  if (next !== undefined && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(next)) return null;
    30	  return m[0];
    31	}
    32	
    33	function stripFences(text) { return text.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, ''); }
    34	
    35	function mdFiles(target) {
    36	  if (!fs.existsSync(target)) return [];
    37	  const st = fs.statSync(target);
    38	  if (st.isFile()) return [target];
    39	  const out = [];
    40	  for (const e of fs.readdirSync(target, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    41	    const p = path.join(target, e.name);
    42	    if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...mdFiles(p));
    43	    else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
    44	  }
    45	  return out.sort();
    46	}
    47	
    48	// Shared scenario scan over one text. Fenced content is documentation; scenarios inside
    49	// DEPRECATED requirement blocks (the marker REMOVED injects) are excluded in every form —
    50	// they are neither demanded nor listed, and their lingering tests become ORPHAN.
    51	function scanText(text, label, idRe, acc) {
    52	  const clean = am.stripDeprecatedBlocks(stripFences(text));
    53	  let m;
    54	  SCENARIO_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    55	  while ((m = SCENARIO_RE.exec(clean)) !== null) {
    56	    const id = leadId(m[1], idRe);
    57	    if (id) {
    58	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
    59	      acc.byId.get(id).push(m[1]);
    60	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
    61	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, m[1]]);
    62	  }
    63	}
    64	
    65	function dupsOf(acc) {
    66	  return [...acc.byId.entries()].filter(([, titles]) => titles.length > 1)
    67	    .map(([id]) => ({ id, files: [...new Set(acc.idFiles.get(id))] }));
    68	}
    69	
    70	// Collect scenarios from spec files.
    71	// → { byId, unidentified:[[file,title]], files, missingTargets:[], duplicates:[{id,files}] }
    72	function collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe) {
    73	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
    74	  const missingTargets = specTargets.filter((t) => !fs.existsSync(t));
    75	  const files = [...new Set(specTargets.flatMap(mdFiles))].sort();
    76	  for (const f of files) scanText(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), f, idRe, acc);
    77	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files, missingTargets, duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
    78	}
    79	
    80	// Same collection over in-memory texts (the projection path) — label = store-relative suffix.
    81	function collectScenariosFromTexts(texts, idRe) {
    82	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
    83	  for (const [label, text] of texts) scanText(text, label, idRe, acc);
    84	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
    85	}
    86	
    87	// ---- TAP lexer (tap-contract): TAP is a version-aware protocol, not a pile of regexes ----
    88	const PLAN_LINE_RE = /^1\.\.(\d+)\s*(?:#.*)?$/;
    89	const POINT_LINE_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)(?:\s|$)/;
    90	const POINT_NUM_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)\s+(\d+)(?:\s|$)/;   // complete numeric token only — `ok 1abc` stays unnumbered
    91	const SUPPORTED_TAP_VERSIONS = new Set([12, 13, 14]);
    92	
    93	// split at the first UNESCAPED '#' (backslash escapes the next char); decode \\ and \# in the
    94	// description ONLY (other backslash pairs stay literal) — so \# is never a directive delimiter
    95	function splitDirective(rest) {
    96	  let desc = '', directive = null;
    97	  for (let i = 0; i < rest.length; i++) {
    98	    const c = rest[i];
    99	    if (c === '\\' && i + 1 < rest.length) { desc += c + rest[i + 1]; i++; continue; }
   100	    if (c === '#') { directive = rest.slice(i + 1); break; }
   101	    desc += c;
   102	  }
   103	  const isSkipTodo = directive !== null && /^\s*(skip|todo)/i.test(directive);
   104	  const decoded = desc.replace(/\\([\\#])/g, '$1').trim();
   105	  return { desc: decoded, isSkipTodo };
   106	}
   107	
   108	// does a text carry TAP-shaped tokens? (misrouted-channel detection — bail-out included)
   109	function looksLikeTap(text) {
   110	  return /^(?:TAP version |(?:not )?ok\b|1\.\.\d)/m.test(text || '') || /^\s*bail out!/im.test(text || '');

===== spec-runner 320-445 =====
   320	function buildChangeProjection(change, cwd) {
   321	  const changesDir = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   322	  const storeRoot = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'specs');
   323	  const projection = { change, modules: [], conflicts: [], unstampedMutations: [] };
   324	  const d = am.discoverDeltas(changesDir, change);
   325	  if (d.errors.length) return { projection, errors: d.errors, texts: null };
   326	  const p = am.buildProjection(storeRoot, d.files, change);
   327	  projection.modules = p.modules;
   328	  projection.conflicts = p.conflicts;
   329	  projection.unstampedMutations = p.unstampedMutations;      // WARN grade — cli prints, verdict untouched
   330	  projection.notes = p.notes;
   331	  const errors = [...p.validation, ...p.hygiene, ...p.casMismatches];
   332	  if (p.conflicts.length) errors.push(`projection has ${p.conflicts.length} merge conflict(s) — no trustworthy projection: ${p.conflicts.join('; ')}`);
   333	  return { projection, errors, texts: errors.length ? null : p.texts };
   334	}

   336	// Full verify run. opts: { specs:[], change, testCmd, idPattern, cwd }
   337	function verify(opts) {
   338	  const idRe = new RegExp(opts.idPattern || DEFAULT_ID);
   339	  let collected, projection = null;
   340	  if (opts.change) {
   341	    const b = buildChangeProjection(opts.change, opts.cwd || '.');
   342	    projection = b.projection;
   343	    if (!b.texts) {
   344	      // failed projection: fail closed WITHOUT running any tests
   345	      const verdict = evaluate(new Map(), [], new Map(), []);
   346	      return { verdict, results: new Map(), fileCount: 0, failCount: 0, scenarioCount: 0,
   347	        missingTargets: [], duplicates: [], bailout: null, noTap: false, tapProblems: [], stderr: '',
   348	        exec: { status: null, signal: null, error: null }, errors: b.errors, projection };
   349	    }
   350	    collected = collectScenariosFromTexts(b.texts, idRe);
   351	  } else {
   352	    collected = collectScenarios(opts.specs, idRe);
   353	  }
   354	  const { byId, unidentified, files, missingTargets, duplicates } = collected;
   355	  const exec = runTestCommand(opts.testCmd, opts.cwd || '.');
   356	  const { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout, plans, points, dupNumbers, tapProblems } = parseTap(exec.out, idRe);
   357	  const verdict = evaluate(byId, unidentified, results, unattributedFailures);
   358	  const failCount = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.fail, 0) + untaggedFails;
   359	  const run = {
   360	    verdict, results, fileCount: files.length, failCount,
   361	    scenarioCount: byId.size + unidentified.length,
   362	    missingTargets, duplicates, bailout, plans, points, dupNumbers,
   363	    noTap: zeroTapParsed(exec.out, results, untagged, unattributedFailures)
   364	      || (!exec.out.trim() && looksLikeTap(exec.stderr)),  // TAP went to stderr — same zero-TAP cure (2>&1)
   365	    tapProblems,
   366	    stderr: exec.stderr || '',
   367	    exec: { status: exec.status, signal: exec.signal, error: exec.error },
   368	  };
   369	  if (projection) run.projection = projection;
   370	  run.errors = infraErrors(run);
   371	  return run;
   372	}
   373	
   374	// machine-consumable shape for a verify run
   375	function verifyJson(run) {
   376	  const { verdict: v, results } = run;
   377	  const clean = v.clean && run.duplicates.length === 0 && run.errors.length === 0;
   378	  const json = projectionless(run, clean);
   379	  if (run.projection) json.projection = run.projection;   // ONLY --change runs carry this field
   380	  return json;
   381	}
   382	
   383	function projectionless(run, clean) {
   384	  const { verdict: v, results } = run;
   385	  return {
   386	    clean,
   387	    result: run.errors.length ? 'ERROR' : (clean ? 'GREEN' : 'GAPS'),
   388	    errors: run.errors,
   389	    specFiles: run.fileCount,
   390	    exec: run.exec,
   391	    duplicates: run.duplicates,
   392	    boundGreen: v.boundGreen.map((id) => ({ id, ...results.get(id) })),
   393	    boundRed: v.boundRed.map((id) => ({ id, ...results.get(id) })),
   394	    unbound: v.unbound,
   395	    orphan: v.orphan.map((id) => ({ id, ...results.get(id) })),
   396	    unidentified: v.unidentified.map(([file, title]) => ({ file, title })),
   397	    unattributedFailures: { count: (v.unattributed || []).length, lines: v.unattributed || [] },
   398	    stderr: run.stderr || '',
   399	  };
   400	}

   402	// Fallback test command from apriori/process-config.md's `test-cmd` row (written by init --test-cmd)
   403	function configTestCmd(cwd) {
   404	  const { value, problem } = require('./config').getConfig(cwd, 'test-cmd');
   405	  if (problem) return { error: problem };
   406	  return value;
   407	}

   409	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori verify --specs <dir...> --test-cmd "<cmd>" [--id-pattern <re>] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]\n   or: apriori verify --change <name> --test-cmd "<cmd>" [--id-pattern <re>] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]\n(--test-cmd may be omitted when apriori/process-config.md has a test-cmd row)';
   410	
   411	function cli(argv) {
   412	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'verify', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   413	    flags: { '--specs': 'multi', '--change': 'value', '--test-cmd': 'value', '--id-pattern': 'value', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag' } }, (f) => {
   414	    const a = { specs: f['--specs'] || [], change: f['--change'] || null, testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null,
   415	      idPattern: f['--id-pattern'] || DEFAULT_ID, cwd: f['--cwd'] || '.', json: !!f['--json'] };
   416	    if (a.change && a.specs.length) {
   417	      console.error('verify: --specs cannot be combined with --change — the projection defines the spec set');
   418	      return 2;
   419	    }
   420	    if (!a.testCmd) {
   421	      const c = configTestCmd(a.cwd);                    // init --test-cmd persists it there
   422	      if (c && c.error) { console.error(`verify: ${c.error}`); return 2; }
   423	      a.testCmd = c;
   424	    }
   425	    if ((!a.specs.length && !a.change) || !a.testCmd) { console.error(USAGE); return 2; }
   426	    const run = verify(a);
   427	    if (run.projection)
   428	      for (const sfx of run.projection.unstampedMutations || []) console.error('warning: ' + am.unstampedWarning(sfx));
   429	    if (run.errors.length && !a.json)
   430	      for (const e of run.errors) console.error('error: ' + e);
   431	    console.log(a.json ? JSON.stringify(verifyJson(run), null, 2)
   432	                       : formatReport(run.verdict, run.results, run.fileCount, { duplicates: run.duplicates, errors: run.errors, stderr: run.stderr }));
   433	    if (run.errors.length) return 2;
   434	    return run.verdict.clean && run.duplicates.length === 0 ? 0 : 1;
   435	  });
   436	}

   252	function checkBinding(cwd, name, stage, testCmd) {
   253	  const run = stage === 'in-flight'
   254	    ? verify({ change: name, cwd, testCmd })
   255	    : verify({ specs: [path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'specs')], cwd, testCmd });
   256	  if (run.errors.length) return { infra: run.errors };
   257	  const v = run.verdict;
   258	  const clean = v.clean && run.duplicates.length === 0;
   259	  if (clean) return { check: { id: 'C1', status: 'pass', detail: `verify GREEN (${stage})` }, projection: run.projection };
   260	  const parts = [];
   261	  for (const [label, arr] of [['red', v.boundRed], ['unbound', v.unbound], ['orphan', v.orphan], ['unidentified', v.unidentified], ['unattributed-failures', v.unattributed || []], ['duplicate-IDs', run.duplicates]])
   262	    if (arr.length) parts.push(`${arr.length} ${label}`);
   263	  return { check: { id: 'C1', status: 'blocked', detail: `verify GAPS: ${parts.join(', ')}` }, projection: run.projection };
   264	}

   267	// C7: unstamped mutation deltas are denied by default — a waiver is always visible (GT-16).
   268	// Archived stage: the deltas are already merged; there is nothing left to stamp-check.
   269	function checkCas(projection, stage, noCas, cwd) {
   270	  if (stage === 'archived') return { id: 'C7', status: 'n/a', detail: 'deltas already merged' };
   271	  const um = (projection && projection.unstampedMutations) || [];
   272	  if (!um.length) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'every mutation delta is stamped' };  // nothing to consult — silent pass (GT-16)
   273	  if (noCas) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (--no-cas)' };
   274	  const problem = configCasProblem(cwd);                       // consumption-time only
   275	  if (problem) return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked', detail: `${problem} — a broken config never equals a waiver` };
   276	  if (configCas(cwd) === 'optional') return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (process-config cas: optional)' };
   277	  return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked',
   278	    detail: `unstamped mutation delta(s): ${um.join(', ')} — run: apriori stamp <store-file> (or waive with --no-cas / a process-config cas: optional row)` };
   279	}
   280	
   281	// The whole evaluation. → { code, stage, checks, result, blocked, errors }
   282	function runGate(opts) {
   283	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
   284	  const res = { code: 0, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'PASS', blocked: 0, errors: [], change: opts.change || null };
   285	  if (!opts.change) return err(res, 'usage: apriori gate --change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--no-cas]');
   286	  const nameCheck = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(opts.change);
   287	  if (!nameCheck.ok) return err(res, `invalid change name '${opts.change}' (${nameCheck.kind}) — bare kebab-case, not date-prefixed, not a reserved name`);
   288	  const loc = resolveChange(cwd, opts.change);
   289	  if (loc.error) return err(res, loc.error);
   290	  res.stage = loc.stage;
   291	  const flowPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md');
   292	  if (!fs.existsSync(flowPath)) return err(res, `no readable flow-state.md at ${flowPath} — tier-aware checks are impossible`);
   293	  let state;
   294	  try { state = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(flowPath, 'utf8')); }
   295	  catch (e) { return err(res, `flow-state unreadable: ${e.message}`); }
   296	  let testCmd = opts.testCmd;
   297	  if (!testCmd) {
   298	    const c = configTestCmd(cwd);
   299	    if (c && c.error) return err(res, `verify: ${c.error}`);
   300	    testCmd = c;
   301	  }
   302	  if (!testCmd) return err(res, 'no test command: pass --test-cmd or add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md');
   303	
   304	  const b = checkBinding(cwd, opts.change, loc.stage, testCmd);
   305	  if (b.infra) { for (const e of b.infra) res.errors.push(`verify: ${e}`); res.result = 'ERROR'; res.code = 2; return res; }
   306	  res.checks.push(b.check);
   307	  const tier = TIER_ENUM.includes(state.tier) ? state.tier : null;
   308	  res.checks.push(checkTasks(loc.dir, tier));
   309	  res.checks.push(checkFlowState(state, opts.change));
   310	  const rdDefect = reviewDirDefect(loc.dir);
   311	  if (rdDefect) {
   312	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C4', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   313	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C5', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   314	  } else {
   315	    res.checks.push(checkLedger(tier, loc.stage, loc.dir));
   316	    res.checks.push(checkEvidence(loc.dir));
   317	  }
   318	
   319	  res.checks.push(checkKb(cwd, loc.dir));
   320	  res.checks.push(checkCas(b.projection, loc.stage, !!opts.noCas, cwd));
   321	
   322	  res.blocked = res.checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'blocked').length;
   323	  res.result = res.blocked ? 'BLOCKED' : 'PASS';
   324	  res.code = res.blocked ? 1 : 0;
   325	  return res;
   326	}
   327	
   328	function toJson(res) {
   329	  return { change: res.change, stage: res.stage, checks: res.checks, result: res.result, blocked: res.blocked, errors: res.errors };
   330	}
   331	
   332	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori gate --change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--no-cas]';
   333	
   334	function cli(argv) {
   335	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'gate', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   336	    flags: { '--change': 'value', '--test-cmd': 'value', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag', '--no-cas': 'flag' },
   337	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ change: null, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'ERROR', blocked: 0, errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   338	    const a = { change: f['--change'] || null, testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null, cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'], noCas: !!f['--no-cas'] };
   339	    const res = runGate(a);
   340	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   341	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('gate: ' + e);
   342	    const mark = { pass: '✓', blocked: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   343	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.status === 'blocked' ? 'BLOCKED — ' : ''}${c.detail}`);
   344	    if (res.code !== 2) console.log(res.code === 0 ? `\nGATE: PASS — ${CAVEAT}` : `\nGATE: BLOCKED (${res.blocked} item(s))`);
   345	    return res.code;
   346	  });
   347	}

   156	// ---- CK-04: every spec scenario carries a bindable ID -----------------------
   157	function checkScenarioIds(specText, name, idPattern = '[A-Z]+-\\d+') {
   158	  const idRe = new RegExp('^(' + idPattern + ')\\b');
   159	  const fails = [];
   160	  // fenced scenarios are documentation, not spec — same rule as the spec-runner (SR-13)
   161	  for (const m of stripFences(specText).matchAll(/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm))
   162	    if (!idRe.test(m[1].trim())) fails.push(`${name}: scenario without a bindable ID: ${m[1].slice(0, 50)}`);
   163	  return fails;
   164	}

   167	const OPENSPEC_MARKERS = [/\/opsx:/, /openspec\//, /\(adapter:/i, /OpenSpec adapter/];
   168	function checkNoOpenspec(name, text) {
   169	  const fails = [];

   171	    if (re.test(line)) { fails.push(`${name}: residual OpenSpec reference: ${line.trim().slice(0, 60)}`); break; }
   172	  return fails;

   272	// ---- cli --------------------------------------------------------------------
   273	// Two modes. CONSUMER (default): checks the project's own spec store (CK-04) and
   274	// scaffolded-runbook freshness (CK-06) — nothing else, so a consumer legitimately using
   275	// OpenSpec or shipping its own README is never failed by apriori's self-checks.
   276	// SELF (--self): additionally runs the apriori repo's handbook checks (EN/CN pairs,
   277	// verdict phrases, codex commands, no-openspec residue) — used by this repo's own CI.
   278	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori check [--specs <dir>] [--self]';
   279	
   280	function cli(argv) {
   281	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'check', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   282	    flags: { '--specs': 'value', '--self': 'flag' } }, (f) => {
   283	    const root = process.cwd();
   284	    const specsDir = f['--specs'] || 'apriori/specs';
   285	    const self = !!f['--self'];
   286	  const fails = [];
   287	
   288	    // CK-04 over the spec store — a missing path is an ERROR, never a silent PASS
   289	    const specsPath = path.join(root, specsDir);
   290	    if (!fs.existsSync(specsPath)) {
   291	      console.error(`error: spec store path does not exist: ${specsDir}` +
   292	        (fs.existsSync(path.join(root, 'apriori')) ? '' : ` — is this an apriori project? (run 'apriori init')`));
   293	      console.log('\nRESULT: ERROR');
   294	      return 2;
   295	    }
   296	    const specFiles = [];
   297	    (function walk(d) {
   298	      for (const e of fs.readdirSync(d, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   299	        const p = path.join(d, e.name);
   300	        if (e.isDirectory()) walk(p); else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) specFiles.push(p);
   301	      }
   302	    })(specsPath);
   303	    for (const f of specFiles) fails.push(...checkScenarioIds(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), path.relative(root, f)));
   304	
   305	    // CK-10: review-evidence secret tripwire (consumer mode; absent dir skips)
   306	    const secrets = checkReviewSecrets(root);
   307	    fails.push(...secrets.fails);
   308	    for (const w of secrets.warns) console.log('! ' + w);
   309	
   310	    if (self) {
   311	      // Handbook checks (apriori repo dogfooding) — run when the pairs exist.
   312	      // CK-08: a ONE-SIDED pair fails (a missing mirror must not skip silently);
   313	      // both sides absent → the pair is skipped (older checkouts unaffected).
   314	      const PAIRS = [['README.md', 'README_cn.md'], ['RUNBOOK.md', 'RUNBOOK_cn.md'], ['VISION.md', 'VISION_cn.md'],
   315	        ['docs/concepts.md', 'docs/concepts_cn.md'], ['docs/legacy.md', 'docs/legacy_cn.md'],
   316	        ['docs/ci.md', 'docs/ci_cn.md'], ['docs/cli.md', 'docs/cli_cn.md'],
   317	        ['docs/troubleshooting.md', 'docs/troubleshooting_cn.md']];
   318	      const files = {};
   319	      for (const [en, cn] of PAIRS) {
   320	        const pe = fs.existsSync(path.join(root, en)), pc = fs.existsSync(path.join(root, cn));
   321	        if (pe !== pc) fails.push(`one-sided EN/CN pair: ${pe ? cn : en} is missing`);
   322	        for (const nm of [en, cn]) {
   323	          const p = path.join(root, nm);
   324	          if (fs.existsSync(p)) files[nm] = fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8');
   325	        }
   326	      }
   327	      for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(files)) {
   328	        fails.push(...checkLinks(path.dirname(path.join(root, name)), name, text));   // CK-09: per-file base
   329	        fails.push(...checkNoOpenspec(name, text));
   330	      }
   331	      for (const [en, cn] of PAIRS) if (files[en] && files[cn]) fails.push(...checkHeadingAlignment(en, files[en], cn, files[cn]));
   332	      if (Object.keys(files).length) fails.push(...checkVerdictPhrases(files));
   333	      if (files['RUNBOOK.md'] && files['RUNBOOK_cn.md']) fails.push(...checkCodexCommands(files['RUNBOOK.md'], files['RUNBOOK_cn.md']));
   334	      if (Object.keys(files).length) fails.push(...checkCodexKnownForms(files));
   335	      fails.push(...checkRunbookVersion(root, files));   // CK-11
   336	    }
   337	
   338	    for (const f of fails) console.log('✗ ' + f);
   339	    for (const w of checkRunbookFreshness(root)) console.log('! ' + w);   // CK-06: warn-only
   340	    console.log('\nRESULT:', fails.length ? `FAIL (${fails.length})` : 'PASS');
   341	    return fails.length ? 1 : 0;
   342	  });
   343	}
